<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-10-hierarchy-brightening-text-block-surface-button-active-states"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 10: Hierarchy Brightening, Text-Block Surface, Button Active States

Applied the design reference hierarchy rule (primary content at maximum brightness), gave log/output areas a distinct terminal surface, and completed the button active-state set. CSS-only — no HTML, JS, or attribute changes.

Changes:

- `styles.css` `.brand-name`: Added `color: var(--grey-50)` — the app name is the top-level identity element; it receives maximum brightness
- `styles.css` `.metric strong`: Added `color: var(--grey-50)` — the large number is the primary data point in a metric card; it receives maximum brightness
- `styles.css` `.panel-heading h2, .panel-heading h3`: Added `color: var(--grey-50)` — panel headings are the primary label on a panel surface; body text inherits `--grey-100` from body, headings stand one step brighter
- `styles.css` `.text-block`: Added `padding: var(--space-4) var(--space-5)`, `background: var(--grey-900)`, `border: 1px solid var(--grey-700)`, `border-radius: 2px` — log and output areas now render as a distinct recessed terminal surface (one shade darker than the `--grey-800` panel background); the Stage 6 scrollbar styling applies automatically to the scrollable overflow
- `styles.css` `.nav-button:active`: `background: var(--grey-700)` — one step darker than the default `--grey-800` surface
- `styles.css` `.secondary-button:active`: `background: var(--grey-700); border-color: var(--blue-400)` — confirms click with both background and border shift
- `styles.css` `.danger-button:active`: `background: var(--red-700); border-color: var(--red-500)` — deepens into the red palette on click; `.primary-button:active` was already defined (Stage 2); all four button classes now have active states

Validation:

```powershell
# Hierarchy colors present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "grey-50" | Where-Object { $_.Line -match "brand-name|metric strong|panel-heading" }
# Returns results for all three.

# Text-block surface present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "\.text-block" -A 8 | Where-Object { $_.Line -match "grey-900|grey-700" }
# Returns background and border lines.

# Active states present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern ":active"
# Returns results for nav-button, secondary-button, danger-button, primary-button.
```

Regression risk: none. Hierarchy color additions only affect elements that previously inherited `--grey-100`; `--grey-50` is one step brighter. Text-block padding/background is additive — the block now has its own surface but the same text content. Active states are new pseudo-class rules that only fire during the click press moment.

<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-8-button-focus-visible-input-hover-selection-color-reduced-motion"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 8: Button Focus-Visible, Input Hover, Selection Color, Reduced Motion

Four accessibility and polish additions to `styles.css`. No HTML, JS, or attribute changes.

Changes:

- `styles.css` `::selection`: `background: var(--blue-700)`; `color: var(--grey-50)` — text selection now uses the dark-mode blue palette instead of the OS default (bright light-blue on dark backgrounds)
- `styles.css` `input:hover, select:hover`: `border-color: var(--grey-400)` — a subtle pre-focus signal; `textarea` intentionally excluded (its resize handle already indicates interactivity)
- `styles.css` `.nav-button:focus-visible, .secondary-button:focus-visible, .danger-button:focus-visible, .primary-button:focus-visible`: `outline: none; box-shadow: var(--focus-ring)` — wires the same blue halo used on inputs to all keyboard-focusable buttons; previously the `--focus-ring` token was defined and used on inputs but buttons had no visible keyboard ring
- `styles.css` `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`: collapses `transition-duration` and `animation-duration` to `0.01ms` with `!important` on all elements — respects the OS accessibility setting for users who experience motion discomfort; ensures the Stage 6 transitions do not fire for those users

Validation:

```powershell
# focus-visible on buttons present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "focus-visible"
# Returns results for button selectors and (existing) tr[data-selectable-row] rule.

# reduced-motion block present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "prefers-reduced-motion"
# Returns 1 result.

# selection rule present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "::selection"
# Returns 1 result.
```

Regression risk: none. All rules are additive. `prefers-reduced-motion` only activates when the user has enabled that OS setting. `::selection` only changes the color of selected text. `input:hover` does not override `input:focus` (focus takes precedence in cascade). Focus-visible rules use the already-established `--focus-ring` token.

## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 7: Empty State HTML Placement

Placed `.empty-state` elements inside every `.table-wrap` in `index.html`. No text, attribute, JS, or CSS changes.

Changes:

- `index.html`: For every `<div class="table-wrap">`, inserted `<div class="empty-state" data-empty-state="hidden" style="display:none">` with two child spans immediately after the `</table>` tag; table-wrap count and empty-state count are equal

Validation:

```powershell
table-wrap count: 97
empty-state count: 97
Match: True
955
```

<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-6-scrollbar-styling-transitions-empty-state-component"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 6: Scrollbar Styling, Transitions, Empty State Component

Three polish additions to `styles.css`. No HTML, JS, or attribute changes.

Changes:

- `styles.css` `::-webkit-scrollbar*`: Custom thin (6px) scrollbar — grey-900 track, grey-600 thumb, grey-500 thumb on hover, rounded corners; replaces the default wide Windows light-mode scrollbar that was visually inconsistent with the dark app shell; applies to all scrollable areas including table wrappers and the sidebar
- `styles.css` `button { transition }`: Added `transition: background 150ms ease, border-color 150ms ease, box-shadow 150ms ease, color 150ms ease` — all buttons (nav, secondary, danger, primary) now interpolate hover and focus changes smoothly; the base transition rule is inheritable so any future button variant picks it up automatically
- `styles.css` `input, select, textarea { transition }`: Added `transition: border-color 150ms ease, box-shadow 150ms ease` — focus ring and error border appear smoothly rather than snapping
- `styles.css` `.empty-state`, `.empty-state-label`, `.empty-state-hint`: New component for zero-data states; centered flex column, 120px minimum height, grey-400/500 muted text; two child elements for a bolded label and a smaller hint line; to be placed by Codex (Stage 7) inside panels and table wrappers that can be empty at runtime

Validation:

```powershell
# Scrollbar rule present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "::-webkit-scrollbar\b"
# Returns 1 result (the main ::-webkit-scrollbar { width/height } block).

# Transition on button present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "transition:.*150ms" | Where-Object { $_.Line -match "button|input|select|textarea" }
# Returns results for button block and input block.

# Empty state present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "\.empty-state\b"
# Returns at least 1 result.
```

Regression risk: low. Scrollbar is cosmetic-only (`::-webkit-scrollbar` does not affect layout). Transitions are additive and do not alter final states. Empty state class is unused until Stage 7 places the HTML elements.

## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 5: Button Type Attribute and Table Header Scope Attribute

Applied two HTML correctness fixes to `index.html` with no visible or behavioral changes.

Changes:

- `index.html`: Added `type="button"` to every `<button>` element that lacked a type attribute — prevents default `type="submit"` semantics on action buttons
- `index.html`: Added `scope="col"` to every `<th>` element that lacked a scope attribute — binds column headers to their columns for accessibility tooling

Validation:

```powershell
# Task 1
Buttons missing type=: 0

# Task 2
real th elements missing scope=: 0

# Note: the literal Stage 5 pattern `<th` also matches `<thead>` in PowerShell Select-String.
# It reports 97 `<thead>` false positives after all real `<th>` elements have scope="col".
```

Regression risk: none. Both changes are additive attribute-only additions with no effect on layout, styling, or JS behavior. JS targets all buttons by ID. CSS selectors do not target `type=` or `scope=` attributes.

<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-4-component-tokens-focus-states-evidence-panel-styling-shadows"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 4: Component Tokens, Focus States, Evidence Panel Styling, Shadows

Added CSS component tokens and applied the remaining interactive/evidence visual rules to `styles.css`. No HTML, JS, or attribute changes.

Changes:

- `styles.css` `:root`: Added three component tokens — `--evidence-bg` (dark-mode purple-tinted surface `hsl(265, 15%, 14%)`), `--focus-ring` (blue hsl-alpha ring), `--focus-ring-error` (red hsl-alpha ring)
- `styles.css` `.metric, .panel`: Added `box-shadow: var(--shadow-1)` — lifts card surfaces off the background
- `styles.css` `.topbar`: Added `box-shadow: var(--shadow-2)` — stronger lift for the persistent topbar
- `styles.css` `.primary-button:hover`: Added `box-shadow: var(--shadow-1)` — hover feedback
- `styles.css` `.nav-button:disabled, .secondary-button:disabled`: Added muted border and text colors so disabled ghost buttons don't look merely faded
- `styles.css` `.danger-button:disabled`: Added muted border/background/text — removes red tint from disabled danger buttons
- `styles.css` `input:focus, select:focus, textarea:focus`: `outline: none`; `border-color: var(--blue-500)`; `box-shadow: var(--focus-ring)` — accessible keyboard focus ring
- `styles.css` `input[data-state="error"]` etc.: Error border color `var(--red-500)` and error focus ring `var(--focus-ring-error)`
- `styles.css` `.panel[data-panel-type="evidence"]`: `background: var(--evidence-bg)`; `border-left: 3px solid var(--purple-700)` — visually distinguishes read-only evidence panels from interactive ones

Validation:

```powershell
# Spot-check: no raw hex in new rules
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,6}" | Where-Object { $_.Line -notmatch "hsl|var\(" }
# Returns 0 results.

# Evidence panel rule present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern 'panel\[data-panel-type="evidence"\]'
# Returns 1 result.

# Focus ring rules present
Select-String -Path styles.css -Pattern "input:focus"
# Returns 1 result.
```

Regression risk: low. All additions are new rules or new properties on existing selectors. No existing declarations were removed or overwritten. The disabled button color overrides work alongside the global `opacity: 0.58` from `button:disabled`. Evidence panel background only applies to panels carrying the attribute, which was set in Stage 1.

<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-2-css-token-system-and-visual-personality-rules"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 2: CSS Token System and Visual Personality Rules

Replaced the ad-hoc hex color system in `styles.css` with the design reference token system and applied all structural personality rules from `V5_UI_DESIGN_REFERENCE.md` Sections 1–7.

Changes:

- `styles.css`: `:root` replaced — 10-shade grey scale, 9-shade blue, green/amber/red/purple status colors (3 shades each plus `--red-900` for dark danger bg), 7-step type scale (`--text-xs` through `--text-2xl`), 14-step spacing scale (`--space-1` through `--space-14`), 5 shadow levels; `color-scheme: dark` retained
- `styles.css`: 26 unique raw hex values replaced with named tokens (full mapping in `V5_UI_IMPL_STAGE2_CLAUDE.md` Task 4)
- `styles.css`: 5 `rgba()` translucent blends replaced — table cell border uses `hsl()` with alpha; selected row switched to solid `var(--blue-800)`; hover/focus-visible use `hsl()` with alpha
- `styles.css`: All `font-size` declarations tokenized — 0 raw px values remain; `font-weight: 650` (non-standard) corrected to `600`
- `styles.css`: Status chips (`state-pill`, `close-readiness`, `refresh-health`) updated — `font-weight: 700→600`, `letter-spacing: 0.04em` added
- `styles.css`: `border-radius: 8px` removed from all containers — panels, topbar, buttons, inputs, canvas, table-wrap, option-panel now use `2px`; status chip pills kept at `999px`
- `styles.css`: Nav active state changed from green to blue — `var(--blue-900)` background, `var(--blue-500)` border, `border-left-width: 3px` left accent added
- `styles.css`: Nav/secondary button hover changed from green accent to `var(--blue-500)`; danger hover now `var(--red-500)` in a separate rule
- `styles.css`: Table `th` updated — `var(--grey-300)` color, `var(--text-xs)`, `font-weight: 600`, `text-transform: uppercase`, `letter-spacing: 0.04em`
- `styles.css`: Selected row changed from translucent green to `var(--blue-800)` background + `3px solid var(--blue-500)` left border on first cell; hover row changed from translucent blue to `var(--grey-700)`
- `styles.css`: `.primary-button` class defined (not yet applied to HTML buttons — Stage 3)
- `styles.css`: All old ad-hoc token names (`--bg`, `--surface`, `--line`, `--text`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--accent-2`, `--warn`, `--danger`) removed entirely — no legacy aliases left

Validation:

```powershell
$css = "apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\styles.css"
# 0 raw hex
(Select-String -Path $css -Pattern '#[0-9a-fA-F]{6}' | Where-Object { $_.Line -notmatch '^\s*/\*' }).Count
# 0 raw font-size
(Select-String -Path $css -Pattern 'font-size:' | Where-Object { $_.Line -notmatch 'var(--text-' }).Count
# 0 border-radius: 8px
(Select-String -Path $css -Pattern 'border-radius:\s*8px').Count
# 0 old token names
Select-String -Path $css -Pattern '--bg:|--muted:|--accent:'
# Nav active uses blue
Select-String -Path $css -Pattern 'nav-button.is-active' -A 3
```

Regression risk: low. The visual appearance shifts — dark backgrounds become slightly cooler-tinted, nav active switches from green to blue, panels lose rounded corners. No functional behavior changes. No HTML structure, id, class, or JS files were modified. The old token names (`--bg` etc.) are gone; any JS that reads CSS custom properties directly would need updating, but no such pattern exists in this codebase.

<a id="2026-05-15---home-sample-validation-policy-reconciliation-handoff"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Home Sample Validation Policy Reconciliation Handoff

Made the Completed saved-policy reconciliation visible at the Home Sample Validation Preview/Append decision point.

Changes:

- Home `Completed Evidence Handoff` now summarizes the selected Completed packet's saved-policy reconciliation status and evidence.
- Home `Acceptance Readiness Gate` now includes a separate `Saved policy reconciliation` row before backend Preview/Append.
- The gate mirrors Completed > Selected Pilot Evidence Packet > Saved policy reconciliation so accepted evidence stays review-only when saved route/size/subtitle/audio/publish policy is not visibly reconciled with the selected Completed output.
- Added exported WebView helpers for Home-level policy reconciliation row/status lookup.
- Preserved all authority boundaries: no Sample Validation append enforcement, no backend route, no mutation command, no media policy, no FFmpeg behavior, no settings persistence, no publish/drain behavior, no rename behavior, no Tk fallback change, and no V4 change.

```powershell

python -m py_compile tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_sample_validation_smoke.py tests\python\desktop\test_webview_real_media_smoke.py tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py

python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_sample_validation_smoke -q

python -m unittest discover tests\python\desktop -q
```

Regression risk: low. This is read-only WebView guidance over already-loaded Completed/Sample Validation evidence. <a id="2026-05-15---sample-validation-real-media-policy-alignment"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Sample Validation Real-Media Policy Alignment

- Added backend-authored `desktop_real_media_policy_alignment.v1` to `/api/sample-validation`.
- The payload reuses saved Settings `settings_media_policy_readiness.v1` and maps it to the real-media pilot categories: H.264 remux/direct-play copy, preferred-language subtitle-to-SRT, audio routing/default language, encode/size policy, and deferred publish/final placement.
- Home Sample Validation now renders policy-alignment status in the existing summary, and the Real-Media Validation Audit treats saved media-policy alignment as a required row before pilot evidence can look ready.
- Added adversarial route-contract coverage for risky saved settings: size guard disabled, TX3G/BDPGS conversion disabled with BDPGS drop enabled, and `AllowNoAudio=True`.
- Preserved backend authority: no route policy, size guard, subtitle conversion, audio behavior, pending publish, settings save, FFmpeg execution, launch, append, repair, rename, manifest write, media file, Tk fallback, or V4 behavior changed.

Validation:

```powershell

node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\crossPageContextView.js
python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_sample_validation_api -q
python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade -q
```

<a id="2026-05-15---command-final-placement-conflict-handoff"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Command Final-Placement Conflict Handoff

Added a read-only command-history and Diagnostics handoff for publish/drain final-placement conflicts. Selected pending-publish, drain, publish, Completed, or missing-output command contexts now correlate against already-loaded Completed/Pending proof rows and show still-pending counts, durable drain-summary proof counts, no-proof missing-output blockers, representative proof text, and a read-first order back to Completed > Completed-to-Pending output proof, Pending Publish, durable drain summary, Run Logs, and Last Stderr.

Touched:

- `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/commandHistory.js`
- `tests/python/desktop/test_webview_command_evidence_smoke.py`
- `docs/CURRENT_PROJECT_STATE.md`
- `docs/V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs/TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs/REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

Why:

- The previous Completed/Pending proof board could identify missing outputs that were still parked or had durable drain-summary proof, but command failures did not carry that final-placement proof into the command-result triage path.
- A failed publish/drain command could still leave the operator jumping manually between Command Results, Diagnostics, Completed, and Pending Publish to decide whether the issue was still-parked output, final-placement conflict, or no-proof data-loss risk.

Safety boundary:

- This is frontend evidence correlation only over already-loaded Completed/Pending proof rows and backend command history.
- It adds no backend route, publish/drain/retry/repair/reconcile command, manifest write, filesystem move/delete, settings save, rerun, cleanup, or media mutation authority.

<a id="2026-05-15---completedpending-final-placement-conflict-proof"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Completed/Pending Final-Placement Conflict Proof

**Goal**

Make missing Completed outputs less ambiguous when Pending Publish or a durable drain summary still contains exact proof for the same source/output.

**Changed**

- Backend publish reconciliation now separates:
  - `missing_output_still_pending`
  - `missing_output_with_drain_proof`
  - `missing_output_without_proof`
- Added backend DTO counts for missing outputs with pending proof and missing outputs with drain proof.
- Backend reconciliation status can now report `review_final_placement`.
- WebView Completed/Pending proof now mirrors the same distinctions with:
  - `completed-missing-output-still-pending`
  - `completed-missing-output-with-drain-proof`
  - `completed-missing-output-no-pending-proof`
- Missing outputs with exact pending/drain proof are warning/final-placement review rows, not clean success rows and not no-proof blockers.
- Pending Publish post-drain proof counts now treat those rows as exact path evidence while still preserving review wording.

## 2026-05-15 - Diagnostics Completed Final-Trust Handoff

**Goal**

<a id="2026-05-15---v5-transition-bug-squash-handoff-archive-glossary-promotion-pg-1-tests-raw-key-triage"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - V5 Transition Bug Squash: Handoff Archive, Glossary Promotion, PG-1 Tests, Raw-Key Triage

### What changed

Executed four deferred V5 transition tasks identified during a full transition health review:

**1. Claude handoff file archive**

The three `CLAUDE_HANDOFF_TRANSITION_SUPPORT_*.md` files were already marked `[COMPLETED]` in `DOCS_INDEX.md` but still sat in `docs/` rather than the archive folder. Moved to `docs/archive/old-ai-directives/` via `mv`; updated `ARCHIVED_MD_INDEX.md` (replaced "needs human confirmation" entries with proper archive descriptions) and `DOCS_INDEX.md` (updated paths to reflect archive location).

**2. Config glossary promotion from draft**

`CONFIG_KEY_GLOSSARY_DRAFT.md` was promoted to `CONFIG_KEY_GLOSSARY.md`. Added 8 missing keys: `CreateTVSubfolder`, `MkvmergeRemuxTimeoutSeconds`, `ConsoleLogLevel`, `FileLogLevel`, `SubSDHTitleKeywords`, `SubSupplementalKeywords`, `WorkerSourcePathMap`, `WorkerConfigOverrides`. The glossary now covers all ~89 config keys from `SETTINGS_KEY_OWNERSHIP_MAP.md`. Draft file removed. `DOCS_INDEX.md` updated to reference `CONFIG_KEY_GLOSSARY.md`.

**3. Tauri PG-1 adversarial close-readiness tests**

Added 5 new Rust unit tests to `lib.rs` covering the adversarial close-readiness cases that were not previously tested:
- `close_readiness_warning_detail_combined_active_work_and_armed_watcher` — active-work reason + armed watcher: all three dialog sections (reason, warnings, watcher) appear
- `close_readiness_watcher_lines_stop_requested_shows_yes` — stop_requested=true renders as "yes"; non-armed status does not add the guard line
- `close_readiness_watcher_lines_error_field_shown` — error field appears; pid=0 and empty deadline produce no lines
- `close_readiness_watcher_lines_empty_status_returns_no_lines` — empty watcher status returns empty Vec
- `request_close_readiness_rejects_wrong_schema_version` — wrong schema version returns error before caller trusts payload

Created `tests/python/desktop/test_tauri_pg1_close_adversarial_scaffold.py` (25 tests) as a Python-side structural verification that all PG-1 handling patterns exist in lib.rs without requiring a Tauri build or window. All 25 pass. Updated `VALIDATION_LADDER_RUNBOOK.md` Rung 5 to include the PG-1 scaffold command and updated `TEST_COVERAGE_MATRIX.md` Critical Gaps to reflect the new coverage.

**4. Settings raw-key triage pass**

Added `WorkerSourcePathMap` and `WorkerConfigOverrides` to `SETTINGS_RAW_KEY_TRIAGE.md`. Both were in `SETTINGS_KEY_OWNERSHIP_MAP.md` as raw-only but missing from the triage doc. Added as priority 5 and 6 (defer until network mode matures). Updated summary count from 6 raw-only to 8 raw-only. Updated `ACTIVE_FIX_CHECKLIST.md` to mark three Medium items complete (handoff reconciliation, config glossary, raw-key triage).

1182 tests passing (all green). The PG-1 scaffold adds 25 new tests.

<a id="2026-05-15---sample-validation-worksheetcutover-boundary-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Sample Validation Worksheet/Cutover Boundary Hardening

**Goal**

Prevent generated real-media worksheets or worksheet-only category plans from making WebView Cutover Gate or Real-Media Sample Set Guide evidence look ready without a current accepted validation record.

**Changed**

- Added explicit `acceptance_evidence_boundary`, `worksheet_run_count`, and `worksheet_sample_count` fields to `desktop_webview_cutover_gate.v1`.
- Added an explicit acceptance boundary to `desktop_real_media_sample_set_guide.v1`.
- Updated Cutover Gate and Sample Set summary text so generated worksheets are described as context/planning evidence only.
- Updated WebView Sample Validation summary rendering to display backend-provided acceptance-boundary text when present.
- Added API coverage proving that a generated worksheet plus current Queue/Completed/Diagnostics proof still leaves Cutover Gate at `pilot-needed`, with zero current accepted records.
- Added API coverage proving worksheet-matched sample-set categories remain `planned`, not `ready`, until a matching accepted validation record remains current.

**Files Modified**

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_sample_validation_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\crossPageContextView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_sample_validation_api.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_sample_validation_smoke.py`
- `docs\CURRENT_PROJECT_STATE.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

**Validation**

- `python -m py_compile src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_sample_validation_policy.py tests\python\desktop\test_sample_validation_api.py`: passed.
- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\crossPageContextView.js`: passed.
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_sample_validation_api -q`: passed.
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_sample_validation_smoke -q`: passed.

**Risk**

Low. This is read-only payload/display hardening plus tests. It does not change Sample Validation append semantics, Launch behavior, FFmpeg/media policy, queue state, pending publish drain, settings persistence, rename apply, Tk fallback, V4, or source/output/scratch handling.

<a id="2026-05-15---webview-hardening-review-unc-normalization-silent-snap-gate-and-smoke-fixes"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - WebView Hardening Review: UNC Normalization, Silent Snap, Gate And Smoke Fixes

### What changed

Worked through all 10 targets in `CLAUDE_REVIEW_TARGETS_RECENT_WEBVIEW_HARDENING.md`. Ran 76 tests (all pass before and after). Applied four patches:

1. **BUG: UNC path normalization (3 JS files)** `crossPageNormalizePath` / `completedProofNormalizePath` / `pendingCompletedCorrelationNormalizePath` used `.replace(/\+/g, '\')` which collapsed the leading `\` of UNC paths to `\`. Fixed by preserving the UNC prefix before the collapse. Applies to `crossPageContextView.js`, `completedView.js`, `pendingPublishView.js`.
2. **BUG: Silent sample-set snap to rows[0]** If the selected sample-set row disappeared after a refresh, `renderSampleValidationSampleSetGuide` silently promoted `rows[0]`, meaning a click of "Use Selected Category" could apply the wrong `category_key`. Fixed: non-empty disappeared key now clears with a visible message; `selectedSampleValidationSampleSet` no longer falls back to `rows[0]` when no key is set.
3. **TEST GAP: Completed/pending proof smoke no-mutation boundary** Upgraded from a blanket `posts.length !== 0` check to an explicit forbidden-route list (matching the sample-validation smoke pattern). Updated the scaffold assertion to expect the forbidden-list guard.
4. **TEST GAP: Tauri startup gate missing pendingPublishView.js** Added `pendingSampleValidationHandoffLines` and `pendingDrainGuardState` fragment checks to `validate_backend_web_ui` in `lib.rs`.

### Remaining wording risk (deferred)

`pendingPublishReadinessStatus()` returns `Ready to drain` at the page-level chip while detail rows use `Ready-looking`. Inconsistent phrasing; deferred as a cosmetic-only change requiring coordinated test updates.

<a id="2026-05-15---remediation_changelog-full-entry-index"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG Full Entry Index

### What changed

Maintained the full Table of Contents in the `## Navigation` section of `REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`, grouped by date (newest first). Each entry is an anchor-linked bullet that jumps directly to the corresponding dated `## H2` heading. The TOC replaces the previous "scroll past this table" stub text and covers all 10 date groups from 2026-05-06 to 2026-05-15.

### Why

The changelog had grown to 26,000+ lines with no click-to-navigate index. With hundreds of entries across 10 date bands the only way to find anything was a text search. The TOC makes any entry reachable in one click.

<a id="2026-05-15---launch-category-evidence-wording-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Launch Category Evidence Wording Hardening

### Problem

Launch category coverage correctly treated non-current Sample Validation category evidence as review/warning, but the headline wording still used `explicit category records` for accepted records regardless of reconciliation state. That made stale or review historical category records look too close to current proof at the Launch start surface.

### Files Changed

- `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/crossPageContextView.js`
- `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/launchView.js`
- `tests/python/desktop/test_webview_browser_launch_queue_readiness_smoke.py`
- `docs/REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Summary

- Home and Launch category coverage text now leads with `current category records`.
- Accepted but non-current category records are now labeled `historical accepted category records`.
- Launch Pilot Category Coverage detail now separates current, historical accepted, stale, and review category record counts.
- The browser-backed Launch/Queue readiness smoke now verifies the normal fixture remains `current category records=0` when reconciliation is review, even though a historical accepted category record exists.
- The same smoke adds a stale-only category fixture and verifies it stays `warning`, with `current category records=0`, `historical accepted category records=1`, and `stale=1`.

### Safety Boundary

Read-only WebView evidence wording and test hardening only. No backend route, sample-validation append behavior, Launch start behavior, queue/completed/pending state, media policy, filesystem mutation, Tk fallback, or V4 behavior changed.

<a id="2026-05-15---sample-validation-records-table-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Sample Validation Records Table Hardening

### Problem

The Sample Validation records table showed `not checked` when a persisted record had no matching reconciliation row, but row detail omitted that current-evidence section entirely. That made a no-reconciliation record look less risky in detail than it looked in the table, and browser coverage did not prove current/stale/no-reconciliation/empty states across the new seven-column table.

### Files Changed

- `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/crossPageContextView.js`
- `tests/python/desktop/test_webview_browser_sample_validation_smoke.py`
- `tests/python/desktop/test_sample_validation_api.py`
- `docs/CURRENT_PROJECT_STATE.md`
- `docs/V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs/TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs/REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Summary

- Sample Validation record detail now explicitly says `Current evidence status: not checked` when no reconciliation row is loaded for a persisted record.
- The detail tells the operator to refresh Sample Validation and compare Queue, Completed, Pending Publish, and Diagnostics before treating the historical note as current proof.
- The browser-backed Sample Validation smoke now renders current, stale, no-reconciliation, and empty records-table states in the real WebView runtime.
- The browser smoke verifies `Current Evidence`/`Gap` cells for `current`/`none`, `stale (warning)`/`2 missing`, and `not checked`, plus stale/no-reconciliation detail text.
- Static API coverage now asserts the seven-column empty-state markup and matching frontend `clearRows(tbody, 7, ...)` path.

### Safety Boundary

Read-only WebView presentation and test hardening only. No backend route, sample-validation append behavior, validation-log write behavior, queue/completed/pending state, media policy, filesystem mutation, Tk fallback, or V4 behavior changed.

### Validation

- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\crossPageContextView.js`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_sample_validation_api -q`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_sample_validation_smoke -q`

<a id="2026-05-15---completedpending-path-evidence-hardening-smokes"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Completed/Pending Path Evidence Hardening Smokes

### Problem

Completed/Pending proof panels already separated exact normalized paths from same-leaf filename hints, but the test suite did not explicitly lock down Windows-share edge cases. A future refactor could accidentally treat duplicate filenames on different folders as publish proof, especially when paths arrive as mixed UNC/backslash and URL-style slash strings.

### Files Changed

- `tests/python/desktop/test_webview_row_detail_smoke.py`
- `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/pendingPublishView.js`
- `docs/CURRENT_PROJECT_STATE.md`
- `docs/V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs/TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs/REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Summary

- Added direct row-detail smoke coverage for UNC-style paths, mixed slash/backslash normalization, and Unicode filenames.
- Added direct Pending Publish Sample Validation handoff fixtures for missing local payload, invalid manifest, unreadable manifest, orphan payload, and diagnostic-error states.
- Fixed the Pending Publish handoff classifier to inspect `state` independently from `diagnostic_status`, so `state=unreadable_manifest` or `state=orphan_payload` cannot render as accepted-after-proof when diagnostic status is generic review text.
- Verified selected Pending Publish correlation reports exact destination, exact source, and same-leaf duplicate-title buckets separately.
- Verified Completed-to-Pending proof rows distinguish exact pending destination, exact pending source, durable drain output proof, and same-leaf advisory rows.
- Verified same-leaf detail text remains duplicate-title/path review guidance, not proof of publish completion.

### Safety Boundary

Read-only WebView evidence hardening only. No backend route contract, Completed manifest semantics, Pending Publish drain behavior, durable drain summary handling, media policy, filesystem mutation, Tk fallback, or V4 behavior changed. The runtime change only makes blocked pending state text more conservative in Sample Validation handoff guidance.

### Validation

- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_row_detail_smoke -q`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_completed_pending_proof_smoke -q`

### Summary

- The digest joins already-loaded Settings BDPGS OCR path evidence, Diagnostics settings handoff rows, and cached Maintenance toolchain evidence.
- Maintenance is not added to the global 4-second refresh loop because the health endpoint can run helper probes; toolchain evidence appears after Maintenance is opened/refreshed by the operator.
- The view is read-only and cannot install tools, edit PATH, save settings, run OCR, launch FFmpeg, drain publish, repair manifests, rewrite sidecars, or mutate media.

<a id="2026-05-15---maintenance-toolchain-readiness-evidence"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Maintenance Toolchain Readiness Evidence

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_workspaces.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\maintenanceView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_facade_maintenance_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\CURRENT_PROJECT_STATE.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Root Problem

Maintenance health rows showed whether tools existed, but the WebView did not explain what each tool actually enables, which missing rows block real pipeline trust, which rows are optional visibility only, or why a clean dry-run is not proof of FFmpeg/subtitle/audio behavior on real media.

### Implementation

- Added backend-authored `desktop_maintenance_toolchain_evidence.v1` to `/api/maintenance`.
- Classified health rows for PowerShell, ffmpeg, ffprobe, MKVToolNix, optional GPU telemetry, and subtitle helper capability.
- Added required/optional source, capability, failure-scope, operator posture, safe-next-action, and no-mutation summary fields.
- Added WebView `Toolchain Readiness` panel and selected-row tool kind/source/capability/failure-scope detail.
- Preserved the existing maintenance health-check route and dry-run command boundaries.
- Did not add install controls, PATH editing, arbitrary tool resolution, media launch, package writes, manifest writes, repair/backfill mutation, or media file mutation.

### Validation

- `python -m py_compile src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance_policy.py src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance.py src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_workspaces.py`
- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\maintenanceView.js`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_facade_maintenance_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_maintenance_workspace_structures_environment_health_rows DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_serves_read_only_web_prototype -q`

### Risks

Low. This is additive read-only evidence over existing health rows. It does not change tool lookup, release dry-run behavior, completed-manifest backfill dry-run behavior, FFmpeg routing, subtitle/audio policy, Tk behavior, or V4.

<a id="2026-05-15---diagnostics-bdpgs-ocr-settings-handoff"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Diagnostics BDPGS OCR Settings Handoff

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics_state_summary.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\diagnosticsStateSummaryView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\ACTIVE_FIX_CHECKLIST.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Root Problem

The Settings page showed saved BDPGS OCR tool/tessdata path evidence, but Diagnostics did not surface that same evidence when OCR was enabled and blocked. Operators investigating subtitle OCR failures could land in Diagnostics logs without knowing the owning fix was in Settings > Subtitles.

### Implementation

- Added a read-only Diagnostics settings handoff for saved BDPGS OCR path evidence.
- `/api/diagnostics/state-summary` now includes `settings_tool_path_evidence` and a synthetic `settings_bdpgs_ocr_paths` issue row only when relevant saved config keys exist and OCR path posture is blocked/review.
- The issue row participates in backend read order with recovery stage, operator guidance, facts, unsafe-if-ignored text, and no open/tail target.
- WebView Diagnostics renders a Settings tool-path handoff summary in the State Artifact Summary and recovery checklist, and row detail points the operator to Settings > Subtitles.
- Diagnostics log row triage now recognizes BDPGS/PGS/OCR/tessdata/PgsToSrt clues and points the operator to the `settings_bdpgs_ocr_paths` state-summary row before treating the line as generic stderr noise.
- Diagnostics still cannot write settings, open arbitrary paths, run OCR, change subtitle policy, launch FFmpeg, repair manifests, or mutate media.

### Validation

- `python -m py_compile src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics_state_summary.py src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics.py`
- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\diagnosticsStateSummaryView.js`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_facade_summarizes_diagnostics_state_artifacts_read_only DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_facade_diagnostics_state_summary_surfaces_blocked_bdpgs_ocr_paths -q`

### Risks

Low. This is additive read-only Diagnostics evidence. It does not change PowerShell OCR behavior, Settings persistence, subtitle conversion, FFmpeg command generation, route policy, Tk behavior, or V4.

<a id="2026-05-15---settings-bdpgs-ocr-path-evidence"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Settings BDPGS OCR Path Evidence

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_workspaces.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_settings.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_settings_policy.py`

- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`

### Root Problem

`BdpgsOcrToolPath` and `BdpgsOcrTessdataPath` were the highest-impact remaining raw-only settings. Operators could enable BDPGS OCR while the tool or tessdata path was stale, missing, or the wrong kind of path, but the WebView Subtitle builder did not show that saved backend path posture beside the OCR toggles.

### Implementation

- Added read-only `tool_path_evidence` to `/api/settings/workspace`.
- Added backend policy helpers that resolve saved BDPGS OCR paths against the pipeline script folder, mirror the PowerShell relative-path convention, optionally account for `AllowSystemTools`, detect file/folder existence, detect `.dll` tool paths that require `dotnet`, and classify status as ready/review/blocked.
- Rendered the evidence beside the BDPGS subtitle controls in WebView Settings.
- Included the evidence in subtitle guidance so staged OCR/drop edits remain visibly separate from saved-config tool-path proof.
- Kept path editing out of WebView; no path picker, arbitrary browser path resolution, OCR execution, config write, or media policy change was added.

### Validation

- `python -m py_compile src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_settings_policy.py src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_settings.py src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_workspaces.py`

### Risks

Low. This is additive read-only settings evidence. It does not change subtitle OCR behavior, PowerShell OCR path resolution, settings persistence, FFmpeg commands, media routing, Tk behavior, or V4.

<a id="2026-05-15---repairreconcile-contract-boundary"></a>
## 2026-05-15 - Repair/Reconcile Contract Boundary

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract_payload.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\contractView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_contract_payload.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\ACTIVE_FIX_CHECKLIST.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`

### Root Problem

Completed and Pending Publish pages correctly warn that repair, reconcile, rerun, and cleanup must remain backend-owned, but the API contract did not spell out what future repair/reconcile commands would require before WebView buttons could safely exist. That left a future risk where a frontend-only "repair" control could be added without dry-run proof, atomic writes, rollback/journal evidence, or clear ownership.

### Implementation

- Added design-only repair/reconcile contracts to `/api/contract` for:
  - `completed.reconcile_manifest`
  - `completed.repair_sidecar_metadata`
  - `pending_publish.repair_manifest`
  - `pending_publish.reconcile_orphan_payloads`
- Each contract publishes `mutation_enabled=false`, `frontend_allowed=false`, candidate command name, owner surface, mutation class, required preconditions, required evidence, rollback requirements, prohibited actions, and a safe next step.
- Added `repair_reconcile_summary` to the contract payload with `status=design_only_no_mutation_routes`.
- Updated WebView API Contract safety review to render a `Repair/reconcile boundary` row from backend contract data.
- Kept route count unchanged and added no repair/reconcile command routes or mutation controls.

### Validation

- `python -m py_compile src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract_payload.py tests\python\desktop\test_api_contract_payload.py`
- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\contractView.js`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_api_contract_payload -q`
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_serves_read_only_web_prototype -q`

### Risks

Low. This is additive contract metadata and read-only WebView display logic. It does not add backend repair routes, filesystem writes, manifest rewrites, sidecar edits, payload moves/deletes, drain behavior, media policy changes, or Tk changes.

- Completed rows now surface recorded sidecar `size_policy` metadata from the backend: mode, routing profile, route reason code, applicable growth limit, limit ratio, actual ratio, exceeded/enforced flags, message, and policy status.

<a id="2026-05-14---schedule-stop-watcher-close-readiness-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Schedule-Stop Watcher Close-Readiness Guard

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_guard.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_schedule_smoke.py`

- `docs\V5_TRANSITION_STATUS_BOARD.md`
- `docs\TLDR.md`

### Implementation

- Close-readiness now treats an armed backend schedule-stop watcher as active work. This prevents the shell from reporting safe close while closing would cancel the future stop-at-schedule-boundary guard.
- `/api/backend/close-readiness` and `/api/backend/shutdown` now surface the watcher-armed reason when the snapshot itself appears idle but the backend watcher is still armed.
- The browser-backed Schedule smoke now verifies that real backend-served Schedule and Launch panels display the current backend watcher state.

### Validation

- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_close_readiness_blocks_while_schedule_stop_watcher_is_armed DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_scheduled_continuous_start_arms_backend_stop_watcher DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_shutdown_warns_when_schedule_stop_watcher_is_armed DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_schedule_smoke -q`: passed.
- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\scheduleView.js`: passed.
- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchReadinessView.js`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-to-medium. This intentionally makes close-readiness more conservative when a scheduled continuous watcher is armed. It does not alter FFmpeg, queue, publish, rename, settings, or schedule-save behavior. Operators may see close/shutdown warnings in a narrow case that previously looked idle, but that is the safer signal because shutting down would remove the backend stop-at-boundary guard.

<a id="2026-05-14---backend-continuous-schedule-stop-watcher"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Backend Continuous Schedule-Stop Watcher

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\schedule_stop_watcher.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_pipeline.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_schedule.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_schedule_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchReadinessView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\scheduleView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_schedule_stop_watcher.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_facade_process_schedule_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_MIGRATION_RISK_REGISTER.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\V5_TRANSITION_STATUS_BOARD.md`
- `docs\TLDR.md`

### Implementation

- Added `ScheduleStopWatcherManager`, a backend-owned daemon watcher that monitors the launched pipeline process and writes the normal `pipeline_stop.flag` when the active schedule window reaches its end.
- The watcher arms only for backend-started `continuous` pipeline runs where schedule enforcement is enabled, `Ignore Schedule` was not selected, and the schedule payload reports a current stop boundary.
- The watcher exits without writing a stop flag if the process finishes first, reports errors if process polling or flag writing fails, and is canceled when the local API server stops.
- Scheduled WebView `continuous` starts are now allowed when the backend watcher is available. `Ignore Schedule` remains a high-review bypass and does not arm the watcher.
- Launch backend preflight now reports watcher readiness instead of treating all scheduled continuous starts as blocked.
- Schedule and Launch WebView copy now explains that the Schedule page can edit schedule state but cannot start or directly arm the watcher; Launch backend preflight/start owns the watcher lifecycle.

### Validation

- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_schedule_stop_watcher DesktopApp.tests.test_facade_process_schedule_policy -q`: passed.
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_pipeline_start_respects_schedule_gate_before_web_launch_ui DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_pipeline_start_allows_scheduled_continuous_with_backend_watcher_available DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_pipeline_start_blocked_by_active_work_does_not_cancel_existing_schedule_watcher DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_launch_preflight_reports_continuous_schedule_stop_watcher_boundary DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_launch_preflight_reports_schedule_and_lock_blocks_without_launching DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_scheduled_continuous_start_arms_backend_stop_watcher -q`: passed.
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_schedule_smoke -q`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This intentionally changes WebView/backend scheduled `continuous` start behavior from blocked to allowed when the backend can arm a stop-at-window-end watcher. It does not change Tk, PowerShell media processing, queue scope, FFmpeg routing, source/output/scratch behavior, or V4. Remaining risk is lifecycle: if the local API process itself is killed before the deadline, no in-process watcher can write a later stop flag.

## 2026-05-14 - Continuous Schedule-Stop Preflight Evidence

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_schedule_policy.py`

### Implementation

- Added a backend-authored `continuous_schedule_stop_watcher` check to pipeline launch preflight.
- Preserved the existing safety behavior: scheduled WebView `continuous` starts remain blocked unless `Ignore Schedule` is explicit because the local API still does not own the stop-at-window-end watcher.
- Added schedule timing evidence (`current_window_end`, `next_allowed_start`, `next_allowed_end`, `next_transition`) to schedule-gate data when available so Launch preflight can explain what stop boundary is relevant.
- Marked `Ignore Schedule` continuous starts as `high review` in read-only preflight evidence. This keeps the backend start route available for deliberate bypasses while warning that automatic stop-at-window-end protection is not owned by the backend yet.
- Updated Launch readiness copy to point operators to the Backend Preflight `Continuous schedule-stop watcher` row before continuous runs.

- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_schedule_smoke -q`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds read-only preflight evidence and timing fields without launching a watcher, changing start-route authority, changing queue/media behavior, or weakening Tk fallback. The main risk is operator interpretation: `Ignore Schedule` is still an intentional bypass, not full scheduled continuous parity.

<a id="2026-05-14---sample-validation-stale-evidence-reconciliation"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Sample Validation Stale-Evidence Reconciliation

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_sample_validation_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\crossPageContextView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_sample_validation_api.py`

### Implementation

- Added backend-authored `desktop_sample_validation_reconciliation.v1` data to `GET /api/sample-validation`.
- The reconciliation payload compares recent validation records against bounded current backend artifacts: Queue snapshot source evidence, Completed manifest source/output proof, Pending Publish manifest/path clues, and recent RunLogs text.
- Recent validation rows are labeled `current`, `review`, `stale`, or `unknown` with exact current-match booleans, missing-current-evidence notes, and safe next action text.
- Accepted exact/partial-path records are now flagged stale when current Completed proof no longer contains the recorded source/output evidence.
- WebView Home now renders reconciliation posture and per-record current-evidence detail in the Sample Validation Record panel. Stale evidence changes the panel status to `Stale evidence`.
- This is read-only stale-evidence comparison. It does not scan arbitrary output shares, accept outputs, repair state, rerun, drain pending publish, rewrite manifests/sidecars, launch work, save settings, rename files, or touch media.

### Validation

- `python -m py_compile src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_sample_validation_policy.py tests\python\desktop\test_sample_validation_api.py tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`: passed.

- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_sample_validation_api DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade -q`: passed (`101` tests).
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_sample_validation_api DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade DesktopApp.tests.test_tauri_shell_scaffold -q`: passed (`156` tests).
- Full desktop suite: `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: passed (`1100` tests).

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The route remains read-only and the WebView only renders backend-authored evidence. Risk is mostly false-positive stale/review wording if historical records are compared after manifests have legitimately rotated or after outputs were intentionally deleted for testing. The text deliberately calls this historical evidence comparison, not processing failure.

### Implementation

- Added backend-owned `GET /api/publish-reconciliation`, returning `desktop_publish_reconciliation.v1`.
- The new read-only policy correlates Completed Manifest rows against current Pending Publish rows and the latest durable pending drain summary using exact normalized output/source paths first and same-leaf duplicate-title hints only as warnings.
- Missing completed outputs without exact pending or durable drain-summary proof are surfaced as blocker rows instead of being treated as implicitly published because Pending Publish is empty.
- Added a manual WebView `Backend Publish Reconciliation` panel on Completed. It does not auto-poll during the normal refresh loop, so it does not double Completed/Pending disk scans unless the operator asks for the deeper proof view.
- Extended the browser-backed Completed/Pending smoke so a real backend-served WebView invokes the new GET route, renders the backend result, selects a reconciliation row, and still proves no mutation POSTs occur.

### Validation

- `python -m py_compile` for the new backend/API/test files: passed.
- `node --check` for touched WebView assets: passed.
- Targeted Local API/static tests for the reconciliation DTO, endpoint, route effect, DOM IDs, and selectable table helpers: passed.
- `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_completed_pending_proof_smoke -q`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds a bounded, token-protected, read-only Local API endpoint and a manual WebView evidence panel. It does not change queue planning, completed manifest writes, pending publish drain behavior, publish state, media files, FFmpeg policy, Tk fallback, or V4. The main runtime cost is an operator-triggered Completed/Pending/drain-summary read when the manual refresh button is pressed.

<a id="2026-05-14---webview-rename-apply-readiness-ledger"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - WebView Rename Apply Readiness Ledger

### Root Problem

The WebView Rename page already had backend-owned preview/apply, checked-row scope, path ordering, selection audit, bulk overrides, review board, and last-apply evidence. The remaining operator-trust gap was the exact pre-apply risk posture: an operator could see the selected scope text, but duplicate destinations, existing target collisions, stale preview/error states, sidecar/force impact, and TV ordering assumptions were not summarized in one concise ledger immediately before the filesystem mutation button.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_rename_readiness_smoke.py`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\TLDR.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a read-only `Apply Readiness` table before `Apply Checked / Selected Rename`.
- The ledger evaluates backend preview availability, exact checked/selected `selected_sources` scope, blocked rows, duplicate target destinations, existing target collisions, warnings/matches, TV ordering or movie scrub review, sidecar move count, force-pipeline rows, final-name overrides, and the backend-only mutation boundary.
- Empty-path and preview-error refreshes now clear stale preview rows, selected row keys, and checked-row keys before recomputing selection/readiness evidence.
- Added a Node-backed WebView rename readiness smoke that verifies a ready single-row scope and a blocked duplicate-target scope without calling `rename.apply`.
- Added a browser-backed Rename smoke root wrapper and unittest. It loads the real backend-served WebView in installed Chrome/Edge headless, clicks actual Rename rows and Check Applicable Rows, verifies Apply Readiness status, and proves duplicate-target blocking does not post to `rename.apply`.
- Tightened WebView apply handling so duplicate destination targets and existing destination collisions shown as readiness blockers stop before confirmation/backend POST. Backend apply validation remains authoritative.
- Added the browser rename smoke to the release self-test layout gate and docs index.

### Validation

- Ran JavaScript syntax check for `renameView.js`: passed.
- Ran `python -m py_compile tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py tests\python\desktop\test_webview_rename_readiness_smoke.py`: passed.

<a id="2026-05-14---webview-settings-staged-media-policy-delta"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - WebView Settings Staged Media Policy Delta

### Root Problem

The WebView Settings page already had structured builders, saved backend media-policy readiness, local patch impact, save readiness, launch impact, and backend preview/save evidence. The remaining operator-trust gap was the exact staged Changes JSON candidate: if the JSON was edited manually or merged from several builders, the operator still had to infer how that candidate differed from the saved config across routing, video, subtitles, audio, publish safety, runtime, and network posture before backend preview/save.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\settingsView.js`

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`

- `docs\TLDR.md`

### Implementation

- Added a read-only `Staged Media Policy Delta` panel inside Settings Patch Preview.
- The panel compares current saved config values with the current local Changes JSON candidate.
- Delta rows cover patch scope, routing/size guard, video route/encoder precision, container/subtitle preservation, audio direct-play predictability, publish/source safety, runtime/diagnostics guardrails, network/worker policy, and mutation boundary.
- The panel flags blocked/review states for unknown keys, source deletion, drop-without-convert subtitle conflicts, no-audio output, empty custom audio codec lists, MP4 container review, raw FFmpeg flags, skipped stability/integrity checks, PATH tool fallback, short encode timeout, zero log retention, and network role/override changes.
- Invalid Changes JSON now clears the delta table and explains that backend preview/save cannot run until the JSON is valid.
- Launch unsaved-settings warnings and the Saved Settings vs Launch Intent checklist now echo the staged media-policy delta status and the first local review row, while still stating that Launch uses saved backend settings only.
- The Tauri pre-window WebView asset gate now verifies the staged policy delta index and script fragments before opening the preview shell.

Daily-use review needs direct, read-only views for common high-risk states like launch blockers, missing outputs, size growth, do-not-drain rows, invalid manifests, and route-specific checks.

### Implementation

- Added shared display-only investigation-filter helpers and updated filter summaries to include the active investigation view.
- Queue now has investigation views for launch blockers, runtime failed, deferred checks, priority rows, encode routes, remux routes, and TV parse review.
- Completed now has investigation views for missing output, size growth, sidecar issues, runtime failed, encode routes, remux routes, and route review.
- Pending Publish now has investigation views for do-not-drain, missing payload, invalid/unreadable manifest, missing sidecars, orphan payload, and ready-to-drain.
- The same hidden-review guardrail now applies when an investigation view hides blocked/warning rows.
- Browser and Node row-detail smokes now prove investigation filters warn when high-risk rows are hidden.

<a id="2026-05-14---webview-queuecompletedpending-status-filters"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - WebView Queue/Completed/Pending Status Filters

### Root Problem

The new filter summaries warned when text filters hid review rows, but operators still had no first-class way to narrow Queue, Completed, or Pending Publish tables by risk state. Searching for terms like `blocked`, `missing`, or `do_not_drain` is brittle because those signals are spread across backend severity, health, drain recommendation, review flags, and row guidance fields.

- Completed status filtering uses output health, missing output, integrity issues, size growth, backend severity, and consistency review reasons.
- Pending Publish status filtering uses do-not-drain recommendations, diagnostic severity, missing payload state, ready-to-drain state, and recovery/review reasons.
- Filter summaries now include both text filter and status filter state, visible status mix, hidden review-row count, display cap warning, and mutation boundary.
- Browser and Node row-detail smokes now prove both no-match text filters and ready-only status filters warn when high-risk rows are hidden.

<a id="2026-05-14---webview-queuecompletedpending-filter-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - WebView Queue/Completed/Pending Filter Guardrails

### Root Problem

Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish already had selectable high-risk rows and diagnostics handoffs, but the main table filters could hide blocked/warning rows while leaving the page looking cleaner. That is risky for daily use because an operator could make a launch, rerun/cleanup, or publish/drain decision from a filtered view without realizing review rows were hidden.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\domHelpers.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`

### Implementation

- Added shared read-only filter-summary helpers that count visible row statuses and warn when filters hide blocked/warning rows.
- Added `Queue filter`, `Completed filter`, and `Pending publish filter` summary panels beside the existing table filters.
- Queue now classifies filtered rows as blocked/warning/ready using backend severity, block reasons, runtime issues, priority/review flags, and row review reasons.
- Completed now classifies filtered rows as blocked/warning/match using missing outputs, integrity issues, size growth, backend severity, and consistency review reasons.
- Pending Publish now classifies filtered rows as failed/warning/match using do-not-drain recommendations, diagnostic severity, missing payloads, ready-to-drain state, and row review reasons.
- The browser diagnostics handoff smoke now verifies real browser filter behavior: after clicking actual Queue/Completed/Pending rows, it applies no-match filters and asserts hidden-review guardrails appear before diagnostics handoff continues.

<a id="2026-05-14---browser-backed-diagnostics-handoff-smoke"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Browser-Backed Diagnostics Handoff Smoke

### Root Problem

The browser high-risk smoke proved selected-row guidance could render in Chrome/Edge, but it did not prove daily-driver diagnostics controls could be clicked in the real browser runtime. Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish depend on those controls for operator trust: bridge to Diagnostics, read bounded logs, and inspect backend-allowlisted artifacts without moving mutation authority into WebView.

### Files Changed

- `Test-WebViewBrowserDiagnosticsHandoffSmoke.ps1`
- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_diagnostics_handoff_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\README.md`
- `docs\DOCS_INDEX.md`
- `docs\TLDR.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a browser-backed smoke that starts a temporary token-protected local API against backend-produced high-risk Queue, Completed, Pending Publish, and runtime-event fixture state.
- The test launches installed Chrome/Edge headless through Chrome DevTools Protocol using Node's built-in `WebSocket`; no Playwright or Puppeteer dependency is added.
- The browser loads the real backend-served WebView page, renders backend-produced blocked Queue, broken Completed, and do-not-drain Pending rows, then clicks the existing read-only diagnostics controls.
- Assertions verify the Diagnostics bridge switches to Diagnostics and selects `last_stderr_log` without reading or opening it, bounded Last Stderr tail reads return expected fixture text, and backend-allowlisted `diagnostics.open` commands for Queue Snapshot, Completed Manifest, and Pending Publish produce command-history feedback.
- Added a table-selection path that clicks actual rendered `Queue`, `Completed`, and `Pending Publish` `<tr>` rows before exercising diagnostics handoff controls, so selected-row detail changes are verified from normal table interaction rather than direct render/select calls only.
- Expanded the table-selection path to apply no-match table filters and verify hidden blocked/warning row guardrails before continuing to diagnostics handoff.
- Added a root PowerShell wrapper, release layout coverage, scaffold guardrails, and operator docs.

### Validation

- Added targeted browser and scaffold tests.

<a id="2026-05-14---browser-backed-webview-high-risk-row-smoke"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Browser-Backed WebView High-Risk Row Smoke

### Root Problem

The selected-row runtime smoke proved Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish detail rendering in a Node mocked-DOM environment, but browser-specific execution could still regress due to real DOM behavior, script ordering, page startup, or browser console exceptions. High-risk rows are exactly where the operator needs trustworthy guidance, so the validation ladder needed an actual browser-engine check without adding a heavy test dependency or mutation authority.

### Files Changed

- `Test-WebViewBrowserHighRiskSmoke.ps1`
- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_high_risk_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_real_media_smoke.py`

### Implementation

- Added a browser-backed smoke that starts a temporary token-protected local API against generated state.
- The test launches installed Chrome/Edge headless through Chrome DevTools Protocol using Node's built-in `WebSocket`, so no Playwright or Puppeteer dependency is added.
- The browser loads the real backend-served WebView index and assets, waits for global render functions and detail DOM nodes, then uses the existing render/select functions to inject fixture-backed high-risk rows.
- Assertions verify blocked Queue, broken Completed, and do-not-drain Pending Publish selected-row details show blockers, safe next actions, diagnostics order, and mutation guardrails in the real browser runtime.
- Added a second browser path that writes backend-readable high-risk Queue, Completed, Pending Publish, and runtime-event fixture state to disk, lets the Python facade classify blocked/broken/do-not-drain rows through `/api/queue`, `/api/completed`, and `/api/pending-publish`, then renders those backend-produced rows unchanged in the browser.
- The backend-produced fixture covers a blocked TV parse row with fresh source-lock runtime history, a Completed manifest row with missing output/missing sidecar/oversized output and fresh publish-verification failure, and an unreadable Pending Publish manifest with `do_not_drain`/`manifest_repair` classification.
- Added a data favicon to the WebView index so real browsers do not auto-request unauthorized `/favicon.ico` during smoke runs.
- Added a root PowerShell wrapper, release layout coverage, scaffold guardrails, and operator docs.

<a id="2026-05-14---webview-selected-row-detail-runtime-smoke"></a>

### Root Problem

Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish had strong selected-row detail panels, but the validation ladder did not yet prove those backend-served assets could load together and render row review checklists, real-media traces, diagnostics handoff text, and backend-allowlisted diagnostics guidance from fixture state. <a id="2026-05-13---backend-owned-sample-validation-evidence-log"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Backend-Owned Sample Validation Evidence Log

### Root Problem

The WebView Home worksheet and manual validation template helped the operator compare Queue, Completed, Pending Publish, Diagnostics, and Settings evidence for a known sample, but there was no backend-owned way to keep those observations inside the app. Any persisted record had to be designed carefully so it could not become a force-success, publish, manifest, sidecar, launch, rerun, or media-mutation mechanism.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_sample_validation.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_sample_validation_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\read_payloads_workspace.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added backend-owned `/api/sample-validation/preview`, `/api/sample-validation/append`, and `/api/sample-validation` routes.
- Records append only to `State\Validation\sample_validation_log.jsonl` as strict JSONL through a facade lock. WebView never chooses the write path.
- Preview/append enforce schema, decision, strict JSON size caps, and warnings for weak/missing evidence while keeping the record evidence-only.
- Diagnostics now exposes `sample_validation_log` through the existing allowlisted open/tail/state-summary mechanisms.
- Home now has a `Sample Validation Record` panel that builds a record from the selected/recent sample evidence, previews backend warnings, appends through the backend route, and shows recent records.
- Command history now classifies append results as `Home / Validation` and offers the backend-owned validation log as the related diagnostics artifact.
- Tauri startup asset validation now checks sample-validation panel and route fragments before opening WebView2.
- Release self-test layout now requires the new backend modules.

### Validation

- Added regression tests for auth, preview, append, readback, diagnostics tail/state-summary visibility, command history, route effects, malformed/oversized rejection, and static WebView route usage.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. This introduces a new backend-owned runtime-state write, but it is constrained to `State\Validation`, protected by token auth and the facade lock, and tested not to touch watched source/output/queue/completed/pending files. It does not change Tk, V4, FFmpeg routing, subtitle/audio policy, pending publish, launch acceptance, manifests, sidecars, or media files.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-fixture-backed-real-media-evidence-smoke"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Fixture-Backed Real-Media Evidence Smoke

### Root Problem

The WebView real-media worksheet and Tauri startup asset gate had strong static coverage, but there was no compact deterministic test that started the real local API, served the real WebView assets, and verified that one realistic sample could be traced through Queue, Completed, Pending Publish, Diagnostics, Settings, Commands, and Contract payloads without issuing mutation commands.

### Files Changed

### Implementation

- The fixture verifies exact source/output agreement across Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish, saved media-policy settings needed by the worksheet, command history remaining empty after read-only GETs, and route-contract effects showing read-only evidence endpoints versus mutation routes.
- Added `Test-WebViewRealMediaEvidenceSmoke.ps1` as a root-level operator/developer gate for the fixture smoke. The script resolves the bundled Python runtime when available, states that it uses temporary generated state, and states that it does not process media, launch pipeline commands, publish, rename, save settings, or modify source/output/scratch media.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is test coverage only. It improves confidence that the WebView evidence path can render from realistic backend payloads, but it still does not process media or replace real FFmpeg/subtitle/audio/pending-publish validation.

<a id="2026-05-13---tauri-backend-webview-asset-startup-gate"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Tauri Backend WebView Asset Startup Gate

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell validated backend health and route contract before opening WebView2, but it did not prove the backend-served `index.html` and critical WebView JavaScript assets were actually present and coherent. A package could pass backend contract checks while opening a blank or partially broken WebView if static assets drifted after the WebView module split.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\README.md`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `validate_backend_web_ui()` to the Rust Tauri shell startup path after health and route-contract validation, before the WebView window is built.
- The gate fetches the backend-served `/`, `/assets/app.js`, and `/assets/crossPageContextView.js` through the same local backend request path and verifies critical fragments for bootstrap injection, Home/Settings/Diagnostics panels, the real-media validation worksheet, refresh coordination, settings handoff, and cross-page worksheet helpers.
- Added Rust failure-injection tests for valid assets and missing real-media worksheet fragments, including no-token-leak checks in error strings.
- Updated Tauri scaffold tests and README wording so the launch smoke is documented as backend + WebView asset validation, not only process/window lifecycle validation.

### Validation

- Added static Python coverage for the new Rust startup gate and Rust unit-test names.
- Ran Rust unit tests, focused Tauri scaffold/static tests, the full desktop suite, Tauri build checks, release self-test, and the live Tauri launch/close smoke listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. This is a startup gate, so it can stop Tauri preview launch earlier if static assets are missing or stale. That is deliberate: it fails before opening a broken shell. It does not change Tk, V4, backend command contracts, media policy, filesystem mutation, or local API route behavior.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-queuecompleted-route-agreement"></a>

### Root Problem

Queue and Completed had strong row-level proof, but operators still had to manually compare a completed manifest row against the current Queue payload to answer whether a completed source was still runnable, whether a queued route contradicted completed route evidence, or whether a same-filename match was only a duplicate-title hint.

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`
