- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a `Queue / Completed Route Agreement` panel to Completed.
- Added read-only comparison rows for completed route-proof coverage, exact completed-source/current-queue source overlaps, route mismatches, reason mismatches, same-filename review hints, no-overlap state, and aggregate agreement summary.
- Added selected-row detail that shows completed source/output/route/size proof beside queue source/route/status proof and bounded route-evidence lines from each side.
- Exported current Queue payload/rows through `getLastQueuePayload()` and `getLastQueueRows()` so Completed can compare loaded backend payloads without owning queue mutation or route planning.
- Kept the comparison read-only. No launch, rerun, drain, repair, reconcile, deletion, manifest-write, queue mutation, media policy, Tk, or V4 behavior changed.

### Validation

- Added static WebView coverage for the new panel, queue exports, route agreement helpers, exact-overlap/mismatch wording, and mutation guardrails.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks and the targeted WebView/local API static test. Broader suite results are listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is read-only correlation over already-loaded `/api/queue` and `/api/completed` payloads. Same-filename matches are explicitly labeled as weak hints, and exact path overlaps are presented as review evidence rather than automatic repair/reprocess decisions.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-pending-publish-drain-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Pending Publish Drain Guard

### Root Problem

Pending Publish already had a read-only drain decision checklist, recovery dry-run planning, drain confidence, durable summary, and diagnostics handoff. The actual `Publish Parked Outputs` button still used a generic confirmation prompt, so an operator could click through without the final action reflecting the loaded "Do not drain", review, or evidence-incomplete state.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a `Publish Button Guard` panel immediately above the Pending Publish drain button.
- Added `pendingDrainGuardState()`, `pendingDrainGuardLines()`, and `renderPendingDrainGuard()` to convert the existing drain decision checklist into an action-level guard.
- Locally blocks the WebView drain click when loaded evidence says `Do not drain`, `Evidence incomplete`, or `Not evaluated`; the blocked attempt is recorded as a warning command result and no backend POST is sent.
- Uses a stronger confirmation message for allowed/review drain attempts, including current decision status, row counts, issue counts, missing payload references, durable drain summary status, and latest drain command evidence.
- Preserved `/api/pipeline/start` as the only drain mutation path. The frontend guard does not move, publish, repair, rewrite, delete, accept outputs, write manifests, bypass backend validation, or change pending-publish policy.

### Validation

- Added static WebView coverage for the guard panel, guard helper functions, local blocked-result data, and review confirmation text.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks for `pendingPublishView.js` and `launchView.js`.
- Ran the targeted WebView/local API static test. Broader suite results are listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. This adds a frontend guard before an existing backend-owned mutation route. It can prevent an operator from starting a drain from WebView when loaded evidence is blocked or incomplete, but it does not weaken backend validation or remove the Tk fallback.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The backend route already existed and remains token-protected; the new WebView control is frontend-gated to safe close-readiness only. The main risk is operator confusion between closing the WebView window and explicit backend shutdown, so the panel states both the guardrail and Tauri-window behavior.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-real-media-validation-boundary-guidance"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Real-Media Validation Boundary Guidance

### Root Problem

The WebView preview path had strong launch, diagnostics, and maintenance checks, but those checks could still be misread as proof that a specific media file would route, subtitle-convert, audio-select, publish, and stay within size policy correctly. The operator needed clearer in-app wording that preview/build/release dry-run confidence is shell/package confidence, not real-media proof.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\diagnosticsView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\maintenanceView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added Launch real-media readiness guidance for pipeline and CSV rerun preflights, plus Audit-specific wording that audit evidence applies after files exist.
- Added a Launch risk-handoff row and summary line that distinguish saved-settings/start-readiness checks from completed sample-run proof.
- Added Diagnostics investigation/log guidance explaining that clean or missing logs are not proof of FFmpeg route correctness, subtitle OCR/SRT output, audio selection, size guards, source stability, or publish completion.
- Added Maintenance readiness and dry-run confidence wording explaining that ops/release/metadata/backfill dry runs prove packaging/backfill posture only.
- Added dry-run result text for release and completed-manifest backfill commands so successful dry runs do not imply real-media validation.
- No backend routes, process starts, repair controls, queue mutation, publish mutation, Network lifecycle controls, media policy, Tk behavior, or V4 behavior changed.

### Validation

- Added WebView static coverage for the new Launch, Diagnostics, and Maintenance helper functions and boundary wording.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks and targeted WebView/static tests. Broader suite results are listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is presentation-only guidance over existing backend data and command results. The primary risk is operator wording drift, so the static tests preserve the proof-boundary language.

<a id="2026-05-13---launch-diagnostics-retry-guidance"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Launch Diagnostics Retry Guidance

### Root Problem

Launch Command Review could correlate a failed start command with cached preflight/checklist evidence, but it still left the operator to switch into Diagnostics and choose the right artifacts manually. That created a gap between "this was probably blocked by X" and "read these exact backend-allowlisted artifacts before retrying."

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchHistoryView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added Launch Command Diagnostics retry guidance beneath Launch Command Review.
- Selected pipeline/audit/CSV rerun command rows now expose backend-allowlisted Diagnostics buttons using the existing command/diagnostics bridge contracts.
- Pipeline starts add retry-context targets for Last Stderr, Run Logs, Active Jobs, Queue Snapshot, and State.
- Audit starts add Latest Failure and Audit Reports context.
- CSV rerun starts add Latest Failure, Audit Reports, and Failed Reports context.
- Immediate launch result detail now includes read-first/open-next diagnostics target summaries alongside checklist correlation.
- No arbitrary frontend filesystem path, retry command, launch command, queue mutation, media policy, backend route, Tk behavior, or V4 behavior changed.

### Validation

- Added static coverage for the new Launch diagnostics guidance/action nodes, helper functions, allowlisted target additions, retry-rule wording, and immediate result target summary.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks and targeted WebView/static tests. Broader suite results are listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low. This reuses existing backend allowlisted Diagnostics open/tail paths. The only new behavior is presentation and button placement on Launch.

<a id="2026-05-13---launch-command-failure-correlation"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Launch Command Failure Correlation

### Root Problem

Launch showed recent start command results and Diagnostics could drill into failed commands, but the Launch page did not directly answer the operator's next question: did Backend Preflight, Launch intent, or Queue checklist evidence predict this failure, or did runtime state change after the cached preflight?

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchHistoryView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Launch Command Review rows now include a `Checklist Correlation` column.
- Selecting a launch command shows a read-only detail panel that compares the command result with cached Backend Launch Preflight rows, submitted Launch intent rows, and Queue Launch Decision rows.
- Immediate start-result detail now includes the same checklist correlation block, so failed Start responses explain likely preflight/checklist causes before command-history refresh.
- Correlation explicitly distinguishes `Predicted by checklist`, `Evidence incomplete`, `Not predicted by cached checks`, and `No visible mismatch`.
- No backend route, command contract, launch behavior, queue behavior, media policy, Tk behavior, or V4 behavior changed.

### Validation

- Added static coverage for the Launch Command Review detail panel, correlation helpers, correlation wording, Backend Preflight/Queue/Launch intent helper usage, and inline start-result correlation text.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks and targeted WebView/static tests. Broader suite results are listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is read-only interpretation of already loaded command history and cached preflight/checklist data. The primary risk is stale evidence, so the wording repeatedly states that cached correlation is explanatory only and backend start routes remain authoritative.

<a id="2026-05-13---queue-to-launch-backend-preflight-handoff"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Queue-to-Launch Backend Preflight Handoff

### Root Problem

Queue had a strong read-only Launch Decision Checklist, and Launch had a backend-authored preflight panel, but the two were still visually separate. An operator could review Queue and miss that backend Launch would block on schedule, active work, launch locks, saved-settings state, or service availability until they switched pages or pressed Start.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Launch now caches the latest backend-authored `GET /api/launch/preflight` payloads in a read-only WebView helper.
- Queue consumes only the cached pipeline target payload and adds a `Backend launch preflight` checkpoint to the existing Launch Decision Checklist.
- The Queue checkpoint shows whether backend Launch is blocked, needs review, is incomplete, or is ready-looking, with check counts, request mode/schedule evidence, and up to eight non-ready backend checks.
- Launch now displays last-refresh/fetch-failure counts for the backend preflight payload and exposes a read-only `Refresh Backend Preflight` button.
- Queue includes the cached backend preflight refresh timestamp and fetch-failure count in its checklist detail, so stale or incomplete cross-page evidence is visible.
- When Launch refreshes backend preflight data, Queue rerenders its checklist if available so the handoff stays current without Queue owning the fetch or any mutation.
- No route contract, process launch, queue mutation, settings persistence, filesystem mutation, media policy, Tk behavior, or V4 behavior changed.

### Validation

- Added static coverage for the new cached Launch helpers, refresh metadata helper, manual refresh control, Queue backend-preflight checkpoint helpers, decision-rule wording, CSS alignment helper, and cross-page rerender hook.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks and targeted WebView/static tests. Broader suite results are listed in the session report.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is read-only presentation over a cached backend preflight payload. The main risk is stale cached status if Launch has not loaded yet, so Queue explicitly reports `Evidence incomplete` and tells the operator to refresh/open Launch.

<a id="2026-05-13---backend-authored-launch-preflight"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Backend-Authored Launch Preflight

### Root Problem

The Launch page had strong frontend-composed trust panels, but pipeline/audit/CSV rerun preflight text was still mostly WebView-owned synthesis. That made the shell useful but left a boundary gap: operators could see local guidance before start, while the backend launch guards were only visible after pressing a start button and receiving acceptance or rejection.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\read_payloads_status.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\routes_read.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract_read.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `GET /api/launch/preflight`, a read-only route that returns `desktop_launch_preflight.v1`.
- The backend preflight reports pipeline, audit, and CSV rerun checks for target validity, normalized request data, schedule gate, process-launch lock availability, active-work guard, service availability, safety modes, path posture, and runtime-prep boundaries.
- The route intentionally does not reserve locks, launch processes, write control flags, clear stale runtime state, save settings, mutate config, drain pending publish, rename files, or touch media files.
- Added a WebView Backend Launch Preflight panel that fetches pipeline/audit/rerun preflight data from the backend and renders selectable check rows with detail.
- Kept existing start routes authoritative. The start routes still re-check schedule, locks, active work, runtime prep, and service availability at submission time.

### Validation

- Added tests proving backend launch preflight is read-only, does not call the service launch methods, reports invalid mode/mode-policy blocks, reports schedule blocks, reports launch-lock blocks, is exposed through the authenticated local API contract, and is mirrored by the Tauri route contract check.
- Added static coverage for the WebView panel IDs, selectable-row legend, helper functions, route usage, and mutation guardrail.
- Ran Python compile checks, JavaScript syntax checks, focused backend/WebView tests, `DesktopApp.tests.test_tauri_shell_scaffold`, `npm run check`, the full 1021-test desktop suite, and `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The new backend route calls existing read-only schedule, active-work, and lock checks but does not execute runtime prep or launch. The main risk is operator interpretation: the panel explicitly states that backend start routes remain authoritative and may still reject at submission time.

- Added backend settings risk items for empty default audio languages, empty custom audio codec lists, custom/lossless audio passthrough profiles, forced stereo, low channel caps, empty subtitle keep languages, disabled TX3G SRT generation, and disabled BDPGS OCR/SRT generation.
- Expanded WebView local save-readiness checks for disabled TX3G/BDPGS conversion, manual/lossless audio passthrough, and low audio channel caps while keeping backend preview/save authoritative.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-pending-publish-recovery-dry-run-plan"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Pending Publish Recovery Dry-Run Plan

### Root Problem

Pending Publish had increasingly good read-only diagnostics, but the operator still had no backend-owned way to ask "what would I inspect or recover next?" before using the real Publish Parked Outputs drain path. The page also relied on selected-row/open histories but did not expose a non-mutating recovery plan command. While adding that command, focused tests exposed that orphan pending rows with empty manifest fields could lose their leading row-key separator during normalization.

### Implementation

- Added a backend-owned `pending_publish.recovery_plan_dry_run` command at `/api/pending-publish/recovery-plan`.
- The command supports all-row and selected-row scopes, returns `pending_publish_recovery_plan.v1` data, and explicitly reports `dry_run_only=true` and `would_mutate=false`.
- Recovery-plan rows include planned action, trust state, diagnostic status/severity, drain recommendation, evidence fields, proof summary, safe next action, and diagnostics/open target suggestions from backend policy.
- Added WebView Pending Publish controls for "Plan Selected Row" and "Plan All Rows", plus inline plan result and recovery-plan command history.
- Fixed pending-publish row-key normalization so orphan rows whose stable row key starts with the internal separator remain addressable by backend-selected open and recovery-plan commands.

## 2026-05-13 - WebView Diagnostics Bridge For Row Investigations

- Open targets update Diagnostics status text only. The bridge does not open Explorer, read files, launch work, drain pending publish, mutate queue state, or publish outputs.
- Existing backend-owned open/tail buttons remain unchanged for operators who want to execute the allowlisted command directly.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-queuecompletedpending-workflow-next-steps"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Queue/Completed/Pending Workflow Next Steps

### Root Problem

Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish pages had detailed validation, selected-row review, diagnostics links, and command histories, but the operator still had to infer the cross-page order of investigation before launch, rerun, or drain decisions. That made it too easy to inspect one page in isolation and miss the owning evidence on another page, especially for skipped/completed exclusions, missing outputs, deferred publish, stale manifests, and pending-publish issue rows.

### Implementation

- Added read-only Workflow Next Step panels to Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish.
- Added page-specific workflow status/line helpers that summarize the current page's readiness plus the next owning page to inspect.
- Added a Home cross-page investigation-order summary so the operator can see the recommended Diagnostics, Pending Publish, Completed, Queue, and Launch order before opening individual pages.
- Queue guidance now points operators from stale/blocked/empty queue states toward Diagnostics State Artifact Summary, Queue Snapshot, Last Stderr, Active Jobs, Completed exclusions, and Launch only when coherent.
- Completed guidance now points missing-output, size-growth, sidecar, stale-manifest, and warning states toward Completed diagnostics, Pending Publish, Last Stderr, and Queue before rerun.
- Pending Publish guidance now points do-not-drain, missing payload, sidecar, invalid manifest, empty pending-root, and warning states toward Pending diagnostics, Completed, Queue, and backend-owned Publish Parked Outputs only when coherent.
- Preserved backend ownership of all launch, rerun, repair, reconcile, cleanup, and drain actions. The panels are advisory/read-only and use already-loaded backend payloads.

Queue and Completed selected-row details could explain what looked suspicious, but the operator still had to leave the row context and manually find the right Diagnostics artifact. Pending Publish already had row diagnostics cross-links, but its selected-row guidance did not consistently state the order in which to inspect row targets, logs, and stderr. That made real-media triage slower during blocked queue rows, missing outputs, sidecar mismatches, size-growth review, and pending drain failures.

### Implementation

- Added Queue selected-row Diagnostics Cross-Links using backend-allowlisted targets only.
- Added Completed selected-row Diagnostics Cross-Links using backend-allowlisted targets only.
- Queue actions now suggest Queue Snapshot, Run Logs, Last Stderr, Latest Failure, and Active Jobs depending on selected-row blockers, deferred checks, and runtime failure evidence.
- Completed actions now suggest Completed Manifest, Run Logs, Last Stderr, Latest Failure, and Pending Publish depending on missing outputs, consistency issues, size growth, and runtime conflicts.
- Pending Publish selected-row guidance now uses explicit "Selected-row diagnostic order" wording for invalid manifests, missing payloads, missing sidecars, orphan payloads, duplicate targets, and normal review rows.
- Kept all diagnostics interactions behind existing `/api/diagnostics/open` and `/api/diagnostics/tail` target keys. No arbitrary frontend paths or filesystem mutation were added.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Validation

- Added WebView static contract assertions for Queue/Completed diagnostics panels, action renderers, backend allowlist wording, and Pending selected-row diagnostic order text.
- Ran JavaScript syntax checks for touched Web modules.
- Ran focused WebView Local API/static prototype tests, Tauri shell scaffold tests, the application facade suite, and full DesktopApp discovery.

### Regression Risk

Low. The new buttons call existing backend allowlisted diagnostics routes and do not send arbitrary paths. No queue, completed, pending-publish, media-processing, or Tk fallback behavior changed.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-selected-row-review-checklists"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Selected-Row Review Checklists

### Root Problem

Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish row detail panes exposed many raw fields, but the first selected-row view did not consistently answer the operator's practical triage question: whether the row is ready-looking, needs review, is blocked, or should not be drained. That slowed real-media validation and made it easier to miss stale runtime failures, missing outputs, sidecar mismatches, size growth, or pending-publish blockers.

### Implementation

- Added Queue selected-row review checklist text that summarizes row state, operator status, route decision, runtime history, deferred checks, review flags, blockers, runtime issues, and safe next action.
- Added Completed selected-row review checklist text that summarizes output health, consistency, sidecar state, size growth over policy, runtime conflicts, review flags, consistency issues, and safe next action.
- Added Pending Publish selected-row review checklist text that summarizes drain recommendation, diagnostic status/severity, health blockers, recommended open targets, issue summaries, and safe next action.
- Replaced generic unselected detail text with the same checklist structure so empty detail panes tell the operator exactly what selecting a row will provide.
- Kept every selected-row checklist read-only; launch, repair, reconcile, rerun, drain, delete, rewrite, and publish behavior remains backend-owned.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`

- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

- Ran JavaScript syntax checks for touched WebView modules.
- Ran focused WebView Local API/static prototype tests, Tauri shell scaffold tests, the application facade suite, and full DesktopApp discovery.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is presentation-only and consumes fields already present in backend payloads. It does not alter route decisions, completed manifests, pending-publish drain behavior, queue mutation, or Tk fallback.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-queuecompletedpending-validation-checklists"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Queue/Completed/Pending Validation Checklists

### Root Problem

The WebView Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish pages exposed many useful backend facts, but operators still had to mentally combine freshness, runtime history, sidecar/output health, size growth, pending-drain risk, and warning counts before deciding whether a page was trustworthy enough to act on. That made real-media validation harder than it needed to be and increased the chance of rerunning, draining, or investigating the wrong thing.

### Implementation

- Added read-only Validation Checklist panels to Queue, Completed, and Pending Publish.
- Added Queue validation status/lines that combine snapshot freshness, runnable/excluded rows, completed/blocked exclusions, invalid/blocked rows, deferred runtime checks, runtime history freshness, warnings, and launch guidance.
- Added Completed validation status/lines that combine completed-manifest freshness, missing outputs, sidecar health, output/sidecar mismatches, stale sidecars, >5% output-growth rows, unknown-size rows, runtime-history freshness, and rerun/repair guidance.
- Added Pending Publish validation status/lines that combine pending-root state, parked manifests, ready/issue rows, do-not-drain rows, diagnostic errors, invalid/unreadable manifests, missing payload references, missing sidecars, orphan payloads, diagnostic counts, and the latest drain summary state.
- Kept these panels strictly read-only. They do not launch, drain, repair, reconcile, rerun, delete, rewrite, or publish files.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Validation

- Added WebView static contract assertions for the new validation panels and renderer functions.
- Ran focused WebView Local API/static prototype tests.
- Ran Tauri shell scaffold tests.

### Regression Risk

Low. The change is presentation-only and uses already-loaded backend payloads. The main risk is stale or incomplete backend data being interpreted too strongly, which is why every checklist includes freshness wording and mutation guardrails.

<a id="2026-05-13---backend-owned-rename-template-presets"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Backend-Owned Rename Template Presets

### Root Problem

The rename tool had implicit naming formats but did not expose them as a backend-owned contract. Adding richer WebView rename controls without an explicit template boundary risked turning the frontend into a second rename engine, especially for TV batches where operators sometimes want `Show - SxxEyy - Episode Title` and sometimes want `Show - SxxEyy` only.

### Implementation

- Added backend rename template catalog metadata for TV standard, TV no-episode-title, and movie standard naming.
- Added an additive `template_preset` request field to backend rename preview/apply planning.
- Added `active_template` and `template_catalog` to rename preview DTOs.
- Added backend-owned TV no-episode-title behavior that omits the episode title for manual, auto, and pipeline TV preview names.
- Added a WebView Template selector and surfaced the active backend template in the Rename Review Board and row detail.
- Kept all final filesystem mutation behind the existing selected-source `rename.apply` path.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-rename-bulk-override-tools"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Rename Bulk Override Tools

### Root Problem

The WebView Rename page could edit one selected final name at a time and could apply checked rows through the backend, but it lacked a safe PowerRename-style way to stage common batch changes across checked, selected, or all applicable rows. Operators still had to hand-edit repetitive fixes row by row, even when the backend preview already had the correct base names and only needed a controlled final-name adjustment.

### Implementation

- Added a Bulk Final-Name Overrides panel to the WebView Rename page.
- Added scoped bulk staging for checked rows, selected row, or all applicable preview rows.
- Added find/replace, prefix, and suffix staging that starts from the current backend preview final name, edits only the filename stem, preserves extensions, blocks generated path separators, and immediately rebuilds backend preview.
- Added scoped Use Pipeline Names, Force Scope Through Pipeline, Clear Scope Force, and Clear Scope Overrides actions.
- Kept bulk edits as preview overrides only; filesystem mutation still requires the existing backend `rename.apply` selected-source command.

### Validation

- Ran Tauri shell scaffold tests: passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. The new controls influence rename plans, but they do not mutate files directly. Generated names are sent back through backend preview first, and final apply remains confirmation-gated, backend-owned, selected-source scoped, and covered by the existing undo-manifest path.

<a id="2026-05-13---pending-publish-diagnostics-cross-links"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Pending Publish Diagnostics Cross-Links

### Root Problem

Pending Publish rows now carry backend-authored diagnostics, but operators still had to manually decide which Diagnostics target to open or tail for each class of pending issue. That was especially clumsy for unreadable manifests, orphan payloads, missing sidecars, duplicate targets, and missing payloads, where the safe next step is usually a combination of row-specific opens plus backend-allowlisted Run Logs, Last Stderr, Pending Publish, and State targets.

### Implementation

- Added a Pending Publish Diagnostics Cross-Links panel with row-aware open/read actions.
- Kept all cross-link actions behind existing backend diagnostics allowlists: `diagnostics.open` and `diagnostics.tail`.
- Tail actions use existing file-backed targets such as `last_stderr_log` and `latest_failure_report`; folder-backed targets remain open-only.
- Extended Diagnostics artifact drilldown matching so pending publish clues such as orphan payloads, missing sidecars, unreadable manifests, and invalid manifests suggest Pending Publish plus Last Stderr.
- Added WebView static contract coverage for the new Pending Publish cross-link panel and diagnostics drilldown wording.
- Did not add pending repair, manifest rewrite, arbitrary path reads, or new mutation routes.

<a id="2026-05-13---queue-runtime-outcome-history-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Queue Runtime Outcome History Visibility

### Root Problem

The WebView Queue page now explains which runtime checks are deferred until launch, but it still could not show whether the same visible source recently failed, skipped, or completed at runtime. Operators troubleshooting half-copied files, output-path failures, and stale completed history had to manually correlate queue rows with `pipeline_events.jsonl`.

### Implementation

- Reused existing backend `read_pipeline_events_tail()` contract validation rather than adding a second event parser.

<a id="2026-05-13---queue-deferred-runtime-check-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Queue Deferred Runtime Check Visibility

### Root Problem

Some important skip decisions cannot be safely evaluated during frequent WebView queue refreshes. `Test-FileStable` may wait on slow or half-copied sources, and `Test-OutputPathCapability` can probe output paths or network shares. Hiding those checks entirely makes the queue look more certain than it really is; running them during preview would risk UI/API stalls and unintended share probing.

### Implementation

- Added additive queue snapshot row fields:
  - `runtime_checks_deferred`
  - `runtime_check_codes`
  - `runtime_check_notes`
- Marked unblocked runnable rows with deferred runtime checks for:
  - `source_stability`
  - `output_path_capability`
- Added Python contract parsing, facade row metadata, aggregate `runtime_check_deferred_count`, and `runtime_check_code_counts`.
- Rendered deferred runtime checks in WebView Queue summary, readiness, breakdown, and row detail.
- Kept file stability and output path capability as runtime-only checks; the queue preview does not wait on source stability or touch output paths.

### Files Changed

- `Pipeline\Modules\PipelineEngine.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_queue_plan_snapshot.schema.json`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\queue_snapshot.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_inventory.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_queue_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Validation

- PowerShell parser and focused queue/contract/WebView tests passed before broader validation.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is visibility-only metadata. It does not change `Test-FileStable`, `Test-OutputPathCapability`, queue execution order, output probing, or media policy.

<a id="2026-05-13---queue-deterministic-preflight-block-codes"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Queue Deterministic Preflight Block Codes

### Root Problem

The queue snapshot could mark rows as blocked, but it only carried free-form blocked text. It also did not mirror several cheap terminal `Process-File` preflight decisions, so the WebView could show rows as generally review-needed instead of explaining that the runtime would skip them for a bad extension, terminal failure marker, or TV parse problem.

### Implementation

- Added `Get-QueuePlanPreflightBlock` to the PowerShell queue snapshot builder.
- Mirrored cheap deterministic `Process-File` preflight terminal skips in queue snapshot rows:
  - bad source extension: `bad_extension`
  - permanent failure marker: `source_failure_permanent`
  - operator-required failure marker: `source_failure_operator_required`
  - unreliable TV parse: `tv_parse_unreliable`
  - already-processed check exception: `already_processed_check_failed`
- Added additive `blocked_reason_code` to queue snapshot rows, schema validation, Python contract parsing, facade rows, route evidence, and WebView details.
- Added aggregate `blocked_reason_code_counts` to `/api/queue` and the WebView Queue Breakdown panel.
- Left slower runtime-only checks such as file stability and output path capability out of snapshot generation to avoid blocking queue preview or probing output paths during a read-only view.

### Files Changed

- `Pipeline\Modules\PipelineEngine.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_queue_plan_snapshot.schema.json`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\queue_snapshot.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_inventory.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_queue_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\queueView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Validation

- PowerShell parser, focused contract/facade/WebView tests, Tauri/WebView build checks, and full desktop discovery were run for this batch.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes queue snapshot visibility only. It does not change `Process-File`, source scanning, already-processed decisions, file stability waits, output path probes, queue execution order, or media policy.

<a id="2026-05-13---queue-excluded-row-open-actions-and-blocked-row-clarity"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Queue Excluded Row Open Actions And Blocked-Row Clarity

### Root Problem

The WebView Queue page could show backend-authored excluded rows, but the operator could not open those backend-selected source paths from the UI. It also used aggregate completed/blocked wording without separately counting visible blocked rows, which made it too easy to confuse rows filtered before the runnable list with rows still visible but blocked by `blocked_reason`.

- Added WebView selection, detail, and open controls for excluded source rows.
- Added visible blocked-row count and blocked-reason counts to the queue preview DTO and WebView summary/breakdown.
- Updated the Local API contract table to display allowed row scopes.

Low. The route remains allowlisted and backend-owned, with no frontend path mutation or queue mutation. The new `row_scope` defaults to `runnable`, preserving existing WebView/Tk route behavior.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-completed-and-pending-publish-risk-breakdowns"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Completed And Pending Publish Risk Breakdowns

### Root Problem

The WebView Completed and Pending Publish pages had row tables and basic readiness text, but operators still had to inspect individual rows to answer high-trust questions: "Which routes produced these outputs?", "Which rows grew beyond the source size?", "How many parked outputs are actually drain-ready?", and "Are pending rows blocked by missing manifests, payloads, or sidecars?" Those answers are especially important after oversized encode investigations and deferred-publish recovery.

### Implementation

- Added backend-owned Completed preview aggregates for route, publish state, output health, media type, audio/subtitle decision totals, unknown size comparisons, output growth rows, and rows over +5% growth.
- Added per-row Completed output delta fields so WebView can flag likely oversized outputs without changing remux/encode policy.
- Added backend-owned Pending Publish aggregates for state, route, drain-ready rows, issue rows, orphan payloads, invalid/unreadable manifests, and missing sidecars.
- Added per-row Pending Publish `ready_to_drain` and `issue_summary` fields so the WebView can explain why a row needs review before drain.
- Added WebView Completed "Route / Publish Breakdown" and Pending Publish "Risk Breakdown" panels.
- Updated Completed and Pending row details to show output growth and pending row readiness/issue summaries.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto_inventory.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_completed_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_pending_publish_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`

### Validation

- Added facade contract assertions for Completed route/publish/health/media-type/decision/size-growth fields.
- Added facade contract assertions for Pending Publish state/route/ready/issue/orphan/invalid/missing-sidecar fields.
- Added WebView static coverage for the new Completed and Pending panel hooks and rendering functions.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is additive read-only metadata and presentation over existing manifest/scan payloads. It does not add repair, drain, filesystem mutation, or media-policy behavior. The main risk is stale manifests with missing `source_size`/`output_size`; those are counted as unknown instead of blocking display.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-rename-selection-audit-and-apply-result-detail"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Rename Selection Audit And Apply Result Detail

### Root Problem

The Rename page could preview and apply selected rows safely, but the operator still had to mentally reconstruct the exact apply scope and post-apply outcome from row checkboxes plus command history. For batch movie/TV edits, that is too easy to misread, especially when rows include matches, warnings, sidecar moves, force-through-pipeline flags, or case-only renames.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_rename_planner.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_rename_apply_runner.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_rename_policy.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameHistoryView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added backend rename preview metadata for change kind, source/destination parent folders, destination existence, and planned sidecar count.
- Added backend rename apply summary counts for selected rows, unchanged rows, media operations, sidecar operations, and sidecar moves.
- Added a WebView Rename Selection Audit panel that summarizes the exact checked/selected apply scope, statuses, rename/case-only/unchanged actions, sidecar moves, force-through-pipeline rows, duplicate destinations, warnings, and errors.
- Added a WebView Last Apply Result panel that renders backend apply counts, undo manifest, row outcomes, warnings, and errors.
- Kept filesystem mutation entirely behind `/api/rename/apply`; the frontend still sends explicit `selected_sources` only.

### Validation

- Added preview/apply assertions for the new backend metadata.
- Added WebView static assertions for the new panels, renderer functions, and guarded apply-result display.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The actual rename transaction path is unchanged, but backend preview/apply payloads now include extra metadata. Existing consumers should ignore unknown fields; the WebView uses the new fields for operator clarity only.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-diagnostics-state-artifact-summary"></a>

### Root Problem

Diagnostics already exposed recent errors, ActiveJobs, progress, open actions, and bounded file tails, but operators still had to jump between panels or Explorer to answer a basic question: "Which important runtime artifacts exist, are malformed, or look stale?" That is operator-trust work and should stay backend-owned because arbitrary frontend file reads would violate the local API boundary.

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\diagnosticsStateSummaryView.js`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added read-only `/api/diagnostics/state-summary` with schema `desktop_diagnostics_state_summary.v1`.
- Summarized backend-allowlisted diagnostics/state targets only; the WebView never sends arbitrary filesystem paths.
- Added bounded metadata/parsing for directories, small JSON files, small JSONL manifests, and small text/log files.
- Large files are summarized by metadata with warnings instead of loaded fully.
- Added a WebView Diagnostics "State Artifact Summary" panel with selectable rows and details for facts, warnings, errors, and recent directory entries.
- Added the new route to the Tauri shell required-contract gate.

### Validation

- Added facade tests for JSON, JSONL, directory, missing-target, and warning summaries.
- Added local API route tests for `/api/diagnostics/state-summary`.
- Added WebView static coverage for panel IDs, asset load order, script content type, exports, route usage, and guardrail text.

### Regression Risk

Low. The route is read-only, bounded, and allowlist-driven. The main risk is false-warning noise from generic JSON/JSONL parsing; it does not mutate pipeline state or media files.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-diagnostics-allowlisted-tail-reader"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Diagnostics Allowlisted Tail Reader

### Root Problem

The WebView Diagnostics page could open backend-allowlisted log and state locations, but an operator still had to leave the app to inspect the text behind common diagnostics targets. Adding a raw path reader would violate the frontend filesystem boundary, so this needed a bounded backend-owned read route that reuses the existing diagnostics target allowlist.

The first pass exposed a manual target selector. This follow-up also connects artifact drilldown hints to the tail reader for file-backed artifacts, so a matched Run Logs, Queue Snapshot, Failure Report, or Cluster Log clue can be read directly without hunting through the selector.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract_read.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\routes_read.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\read_payloads_status.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\diagnosticsView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added read-only GET `/api/diagnostics/tail` with `target` and `max_bytes` query keys.
- Reused the backend diagnostics allowlist/path resolver; the WebView sends a target key only, never a filesystem path.
- Added bounded byte reads with clamped limits, UTF-8 replacement decoding, directory/non-regular-file detection, missing-file warnings, and read/stat error payloads.
- Added a WebView Allowlisted File Tail panel with target and byte-limit selectors plus operator-visible detail/status text.
- Added artifact-drilldown read-tail buttons for file-backed targets while keeping folder-backed targets as open-only actions.
- No shell-open, file mutation, process lifecycle, media policy, or Tk behavior changed.

### Validation

- Added facade tests for allowlisted tail reads, disallowed target rejection, directory target handling, and missing optional targets.
- Added WebView static contract assertions for the new diagnostics tail controls and route usage.

### Regression Risk

Low. The route is read-only, bounded, authenticated, and allowlist-based. The main risk is reading a large active log while another process writes to it; failures are returned as operator-visible payload errors instead of unwinding the local API route.

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\settingsView.js`

- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a File Safety / Publish Patch Builder to the WebView Settings page.
- The builder stages `SourceMovies`, `SourceTV`, `Outsource`, `LocalBase`, `MinFreeSpaceGB`, `OutsourceMinFreeSpaceGB`, `FileStabilityWait`, `CleanupStaleAgeHours`, `DeferredPublish`, `AggressiveEpisodeParsing`, `SkipStabilityCheck`, and `CleanupRemoteStaging`.
- Added guidance that normal pipeline behavior should copy source files to scratch and avoid source mutation.
- Added explicit warnings when staging skip-stability or remote-cleanup toggles.
- Does not write settings directly; Changes JSON still goes through backend preview/save validation.

### Validation

- Focused WebView local API static test: 1 test passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 993 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This exposes high-impact settings in structured controls, but it only stages patch JSON. Backend settings validation/save and existing risk summaries remain the authority.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-rename-checked-row-apply-and-ordering"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Rename Checked-Row Apply And Ordering

### Root Problem

The WebView Rename page could apply one selected detail row through the backend, but Tk supports batch-oriented rename workflows and the backend already accepts multiple explicit `selected_sources`. Operators still had to repeat one-row applies even when they had reviewed a safe subset of TV/movie rows. TV episode numbering also depends on Paths textarea order, but WebView lacked quick ordering controls.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added checked-row selection to the Rename preview table.
- Added Check Applicable Rows and Clear Checked Rows controls.
- Added Move Checked Up, Move Checked Down, and Natural Sort Paths controls for the Paths textarea.
- Apply now sends checked rows as backend `selected_sources`; if no rows are checked, it preserves the previous selected-detail-row behavior.
- Blocked rows cannot be checked, and any blocked selection is rejected before backend submission.
- Backend apply still rebuilds the plan, requires `confirm_apply`, uses the facade apply lock, and owns transactional filesystem mutation.
- Path-order controls only change local text input and tell the operator to rerun backend Preview before applying.

### Validation

- Focused backend rename multi-source apply and WebView local API static tests: 2 tests passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 993 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This exposes an existing backend multi-source contract in the WebView, but the mutation path remains backend-owned. Main risk is operator selection confusion, mitigated by checked-row count, confirmation text, disabled blocked rows, and fallback to single selected-row apply when nothing is checked.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-network-worker-detail-and-filtering"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Network Worker Detail And Filtering

### Root Problem

The WebView Network page showed persisted coordinator/worker rows, but operators still had to infer worker context from a dense table or open raw network state files. That made current file, heartbeat, speed, job identity, status buckets, and extra persisted row fields harder to inspect during worker triage.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\networkView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a read-only worker detail pane to the WebView Network page.
- Persisted worker rows are selectable and retain selection across refreshes when the same worker/job/file identity remains present.
- Detail text shows worker identity, job, current file, source file, stage, progress, heartbeat, speed, return code, error, and bounded extra row fields.
- Worker-row coloring now highlights active/warning, idle/match, and stale/error/failed states.
- Added local-only status and search filters against already-loaded persisted worker rows.
- Does not add coordinator/worker lifecycle controls, frontend filesystem reads, arbitrary path opens, or mutation behavior.

### Validation

- Focused WebView local API static test: 1 test passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 992 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The change only renders existing `/api/network/workers` data in the frontend and does not alter network runtime state, backend routes, process lifecycle behavior, media policy, or Tk fallback behavior.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-rename-apply-history"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Rename Apply History

### Root Problem

The WebView Rename page could apply a selected backend-planned rename and show the immediate result, but after refresh the operator had to inspect global command history to confirm recent rename outcomes. Rename is filesystem-affecting, so the owning page should retain recent backend apply results without changing the mutation contract.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\commandHistory.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a Rename Apply History panel.
- Filters the existing backend/local command stream for `rename.apply`.
- Preserves result status, local-versus-journal source, selected-source count, renamed-row count, optional applied/skipped counts, rollback flag when reported, and backend message.
- Does not record preview refreshes as command history, avoiding noise from safe preview-only requests.
- Does not alter rename preview planning, selected-row apply scope, transaction behavior, sidecar handling, or filesystem mutation policy.

### Validation

- Focused WebView local API static test: 1 test passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 990 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The change only renders existing `rename.apply` command history entries and does not alter rename request payloads, backend preview/apply behavior, or Tk fallback controls.
